Code","volume":"婚后外传：月亮舞会的失宠赞助人","anchor":"chapter-0139","paragraphs":["Mara's invitation to the lunar ball specified silver clothing, quiet shoes and a willingness to be amazed. Cassian arrived with all three and an outsize bouquet that would not fit into the lift. She took one flower, left the rest with the porter and kissed the gift's disappointed owner back into good humour.","The ball occupied a hilltop observatory. Altitude gave the terrace a chill even in summer, but its charm was undeniable. A halo surrounded the moon. Inside, an artist had built a suspended globe whose circumference required twelve metres of silk. Its diameter left very little room for the string quartet. Cassian admired its majesty, then ducked beneath it with less majesty than usual.","The exhibition combined art and nature. Its composition used reclaimed metal from a notorious factory. The density of the darker pieces made one side heavy; a hidden counterweight maintained harmony. Mara admired the intricacy, but not the curator's casualness about the supporting cable. A globe of that magnitude needed more than a novelty knot.","The depth of the anchor worried her more than the silk. In conjunction with the heavy inserts, a shallow fixing could make failure more likely. The mechanic would not arrive for an hour. Until then, Mara asked staff to await his assessment and stop any excess movement. The stewards agreed unanimously to keep the room still.","The curator tried to dazzle her with talk of greatness. She asked about the load test. He said repeated tests had produced slightly different results. Their irreproducibility did not matter, he claimed, because art was inherently unique. Mara disagreed. Distinctiveness of design would not justify uncertainty about a cable.","The curator spoke of aesthetic objectivity with striking confidence, then dismissed the empirical test. Mara disliked the conjunction of certainty and evasion. A safety procedure should not be downgraded into a matter of taste. His inadequacy in this area needed help, not another speech about artistic holiness.","The artist, Nina, hurried over. She had trained at a vocational school and wanted the installation closed until it was checked. A donor had pressed her to downgrade her concerns. He wanted the ball to revitalize the district's expensive image. Its old industrial acreage now supported workshops; his company hoped to buy up the entire site. The lure of riverside apartments had inspired rampant speculation.","Nina had had a bellyful of it. Her decisiveness appeared suddenly: she switched the sculpture to stationary mode. Its momentum diminished. Cassian helped keep people outside its projected path. Inertia made a heavy globe reluctant to stop, though the donor appeared to think money could change physics.","Nina had once asked for leniency after missing a deadline. She had received none. The contrast with her patron, whose mistakes were endlessly forgiven, was beginning to horrify her.","The globe began one final dash around its support. A steward caught a loose ribbon before it touched the lamp. The supporting frame's dominance over the small room had left too little flexibility for visitors. Mara feared the security procedure would overtax staff unless someone reduced numbers. A permissible capacity was a physical limit, not a suggestion.","The visitors complained to no avail. Mara arranged a safe route around the room and asked staff to add some barriers to the entrance. Their effectiveness would be tested by an impatient crowd. A top-down command to behave would be less useful than clear space. The sign-up table had already been overwhelmed; user traffic on the booking page had been mistaken for evidence that the room could accommodate everyone.","Beside Nina's work hung portraits challenging narrow ideas of femininity. Their subjects differed in ethnicity, age, body size and lifestyle. A caption discussed the stigma attached to obesity without pretending a portrait diagnosed anyone's health. The donor called the display inconsistent with glamor. Nina said he was welcome to look elsewhere.","“Your openness is refreshing,” Mara told her.","“My alleged insensitivity is less fashionable,” Nina answered.","The event's sponsor promised diversity and equality but assigned every important speech to men from its own office. Its long report about inequality omitted the resulting inequity in workshop grants. A deficit of accountability could be hidden behind beautiful paper only for so long. The report also mapped population distribution, but omitted who actually used the workshops. Nina had invited craftspeople to ask about their entitlement to promised space. The donor considered their questions an intrusion; the artists considered his intrusiveness in their work worse.","A former volunteer had left after countless unanswered requests about payments. His absence exposed the ineffectiveness of a complaints system run entirely by the donor. The likelihood of a fair answer had seemed slight. Mara heard greed in the terms he offered now, although a harsh impression alone could not establish dishonesty.","One portrait celebrated a woman's naturalization after years of uncertainty. The sitter had moved from outside the European Union and built a local business. Her family stood beside the painting, delighted. Mara watched the donor offer them his most persuasive smile. Charm was his usual answer to a question about legitimacy.","Cassian brought Mara a shawl before her chill deepened. His faithfulness to small discomforts always reached her before grand compliments did. A photographer tried to lure them towards a wall of sponsor logos. Cassian refused with perfect courtesy. His indulgence towards Mara did not extend to making her a decorative endorsement.","He called the old observatory elevating. Mara thought his expression concealed a familiar struggle with mediocrity in catering. The canapé in his hand was inedible, not to mention expensive. She offered him her bread instead and watched the room's supposedly glamorous excess turn into a private joke.","Nina's assistant approached with a quantitative report on the cable. It contradicted the curator's summary strikingly. The test had identified a serious load deficit. This was no dispute about the logicality of a critic's interpretation or the objectiveness of taste. There were measurements.","The donor glanced at the sheet and tried to take it. Mara held it out of reach. His expression lost its halo of benevolence."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1007,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0139\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：月亮舞会的失宠赞助人</div><h1>The Moon Has a Dress Code</h1><p id=\"chapter-0139-p1\">Mara&#x27;s <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4542\" data-words=\"4542\">invitation</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8538\" data-words=\"8538\">to</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4985\" data-words=\"4985\">lunar</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-736\" data-words=\"736\">ball</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7806\" data-words=\"7806,7807\">specified</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7567\" data-words=\"7567\">silver</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1532\" data-words=\"1532\">clothing</a>, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6684\" data-words=\"6684\">quiet</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7501\" data-words=\"7501\">shoes</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> willingness <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8538\" data-words=\"8538\">to</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-792\" data-words=\"792\">be</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-339\" data-words=\"339,338\">amazed</a>. Mara <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3954\" data-words=\"3954\">held</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-4568\" data-words=\"4568\">it</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-5810\" data-words=\"5810\">out of</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-6752\" data-words=\"6752\">reach</a>. <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3941\" data-words=\"3941,3907\">His</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3053\" data-words=\"3053\">expression</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-4961\" data-words=\"4961,4955\">lost</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-4571\" data-words=\"4571,4568\">its</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3780\" data-words=\"3780\">halo</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-5667\" data-words=\"5667\">of</a> benevolence.</p><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"137\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"139\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x015c02","title":"A Duchess without a Halo","volume":"婚后外传：月亮舞会的失宠赞助人","anchor":"chapter-0140","paragraphs":["The donor snatched for the report. Mara made a quick dash behind the sign-up table, where Nina caught the falling glass of water. Cassian stepped between the donor and both women. His charm did not disappear; it acquired a temperature that made the terrace chill seem mild.","The curator called for leniency. His concern had nothing to do with holiness, though a large painting of a saint watched him from the staircase. He feared that closing the display would expose the inadequacy of his preparation. Mara had no desire to horrify the audience. She wanted the cable checked before a rotating moon struck somebody.","The donor's concern for accountability appeared to extend only to people below him. He tried to downgrade the mechanic's criticism before hearing it, to no avail. The curator's practiced charm had given his patron a halo he did not deserve. Nina noticed the likelihood of that halo surviving diminish with every impatient interruption.","The stewards agreed unanimously to await the mechanic. People tend to mistake a confident instruction for a safe one; this time they wanted the measurements first.","Nina showed her how the suspended composition worked. Circumference determined the silk length; diameter determined clearance. The density of its metal inserts affected the balance, while depth of the fixing hole limited the anchor's strength. At this altitude, wind entering the open terrace doors could add another load. These facts made the sculpture's magnitude harder to manage than its apparent lightness suggested.","A mechanic secured the globe in stationary mode. Momentum fell, and inertia no longer carried it across the room. The crowd admired the new harmony without knowing how close the old arrangement had come to failure. The mechanic's decisiveness mattered more than the curator's effort to dazzle visitors with elaborate explanations.","Outside, Nina described the workshops. Their acreage would be worth a fortune if the donor obtained permission for housing. The city's population distribution had shifted towards the river; demand was real. But selling every vocational workspace would not automatically solve the housing problem. It might instead remove work from people who could not afford to leave. A quantitative study should examine both.","It was the notorious plan's scale that mattered, not merely the novelty of the donor's latest slogan. Rampant growth could overtax roads and workshops alike. The city had praised the scheme's majestic entrance, but majesty did not answer questions about access. A low gate might serve a town better than an outsize arch.","The donor accused her of greed. She laughed without humour. “You want to buy up our buildings. We want the rooms we were promised.”","Mara asked to see the grant terms. The document acknowledged the artists' entitlement to leases. Its legitimacy did not depend on whether he still found them attractive. Their diversity, he had written, would revitalize the neighbourhood. Now their actual difference annoyed him. That was a strikingly convenient definition of openness.","His publicity celebrated the European Union partnership that funded the exchange, including artists who had obtained naturalization elsewhere. Yet he had judged applications partly by ethnicity. A report on inequality had omitted this rule. A teacher with a workshop on site called that inequity by design, not a deficit of fashionable language.","Mara listened. Her objectivity could not mean indifference. Nor could objectiveness require pretending every explanation was equally supported. The original terms and the new demands were different. Their conjunction on one evening gave the artists a chance to ask why. She would stick to that plain distinction before alleging dishonesty about every prior decision.","A claim to logicality could not settle who had approved the altered test summary. Its irreproducibility might have innocent causes, but concealing the inconsistency would be dishonesty. The promised leniency should therefore concern a candid mistake, not a demand that everyone stop asking. Mara refused to fit into the role of smiling referee.","The donor changed his tone. Nina should show flexibility, he said. A lifestyle devoted to art could become indulgence. Greatness required patrons; mediocrity complained about them. Mara looked at the installation's intricacy and thought its maker had already earned the right to answer.","“I have had a bellyful of your advice,” Nina said. “My faithfulness is to the work.”","The conversation drew spectators. Some admired the portraits' distinctiveness; others disliked their femininity being presented in so many forms. A visitor made a nasty remark about obesity. Nina's mother answered him with devastating casualness: “The portrait is for looking at. Your manners require more strenuous work.”","Their mother's blunt intervention brought a little warmth back. Nature had given the family strikingly similar smiles. The donor mistook that warmth for surrender and asked Nina to add her name to his revised statement. She refused. His insensitivity had made an ordinary request feel like another assertion of dominance.","Cassian almost choked on his drink. Mara loved him for failing to hide it.","A steward asked guests to await a decision about the sculpture. The temporary security procedure would keep its perimeter clear. Nothing permitted a donor to overtax a young artist's patience indefinitely, but a barrier could at least reduce physical intrusiveness. Its effectiveness was already visible; the previous rope's ineffectiveness had been equally obvious.","The excess of guests had been made worse by delayed arrivals. User traffic on a booking page was inherently different from the number physically present. A top-down decision to open every door might horrify the mechanic. The steward instead asked the next group to wait, a simple permissible response nobody had considered glamorous.","The donor offered Nina another grant if she withdrew her concerns. The lure was substantial. She refused. Behind her, the stationary lunar globe shone with borrowed light. Its glamor survived the loss of rotation. Wait","volume":"婚后外传：月亮舞会的失宠赞助人","anchor":"chapter-0141","paragraphs":["The mechanic finished his inspection while the quartet played beneath a smaller, safer lamp. The sculpture could remain still tonight. Its rotation mechanism would be repaired later, with the load test recorded. Nina signed the plan. Her decisiveness had cost her a patron, but it had protected her work and everyone standing beneath it.","The stationary mode removed momentum from the immediate problem, although the globe's inertia still mattered during handling. The mechanic checked the excess load with Nina. A small funding deficit would delay the repair. Cassian offered to cover it, subject to her approval, then wisely let her decide.","The curator apologized for his casualness. He had admired the composition so much that he had trusted its apparent harmony. Now the figures about density, depth and anchor strength exposed the inadequacy of that judgment. Mara told him admiration was no substitute for checking a cable. His account of the test's irreproducibility would require a written correction.","At the sign-up table, the artists began to gather copies of their leases. The donor complained to no avail. He wanted the spectacle to end before the reporters arrived. The artists wanted accountability. Their entitlement to space would not be settled on a ballroom floor, but neither would it be traded for another evening of flattering speeches.","The effectiveness of preserving copies was already visible. Nobody could lure the artists into arguing about a missing clause now. The old complaints process had demonstrated its ineffectiveness; an independent review might earn legitimacy through better work. The patron requested leniency in public language and received only a promise of accuracy.","Nina's mother had brought a map. It showed the workshops' acreage and the changing population distribution along the river. The magnitude of the planned conversion became easier to grasp when a finger could follow it. A vocational teacher explained that an apprenticeship needed rooms, equipment and people nearby. A scheme could revitalize housing while damaging that network. The conjunction of both aims required actual planning.","The donor offered to downgrade his purchase to half the site. It sounded generous until Nina asked which half. Every building with a working furnace was included. The apparent flexibility concealed the same dominance. Her mother suggested a proper meeting with independent advice. The artists agreed unanimously.","A visitor from the European Union exchange offered to help arrange it. Her naturalization ceremony had taken place last winter, and her portrait hung nearby. She had no wish to become a symbol of the event's holiness. She wanted a working socket and an affordable room. Mara found the practical demand unexpectedly elevating.","The conversation about diversity became more useful when it concerned actual access. Ethnicity should not decide grant eligibility; neither should a patron's preferred femininity. A workshop class on accommodating artists with different bodies had once included a discussion of obesity and equipment design. The donor's adviser had called it unfashionable. Nina intended to keep it.","Inequality in funding could become inequity when access depended on pleasing one man. The participants did not all share a lifestyle or opinion. They did agree that the workshops' nature should include room for disagreement. Their openness was practical: make an appointment, bring the documents, let the people affected speak.","Cassian found Mara beside the lunar display, one hand resting near its measured circumference. He had a replacement drink and an expression of great patience. “You promised me a dance.”","The curve of polished metal tried to dazzle him as he crossed the floor. He made a dash for Mara before she could recruit him for another committee. Behind them, the installation's intricacy caught the light. Her smile, which required no measurement at all, caught his attention more completely.","“I promised to consider the likelihood.”","“That was before I carried a bouquet up this altitude.”","She laughed. He drew her away from the globe's outsize diameter and towards the quiet edge of the floor. Its majesty was impressive; his charm was strikingly more dangerous. She let him see that she knew it. His answer was a kiss just below her ear, brief enough for public decorum and unfair enough to make her forget the quartet's composition.","Nina watched them with amusement, then returned to the mechanic. The objectivity of measurements had saved her from a demand to prove her personal sincerity. She disliked the critic's word objectiveness, but she could live with a dry term when its logicality protected a real decision. Her installation's distinctiveness would remain after the paperwork.","There had been dishonesty in the reported test. Other questions remained unresolved. Mara did not have to turn every inadequacy into greed or every act of insensitivity into a conspiracy. Still, the donor's rampant desire for control was no novelty. The likelihood of a fair settlement would depend on the artists' information as well as his goodwill.","A reporter photographed the notorious donor leaving. Mara did not congratulate herself. Fresh questions would arise after tonight. The map of population distribution, and Nina's resolute response, would be more useful then than another accusation. Countless demands had threatened to overtax everyone. The evening had been strenuous enough without pretending it solved everything, not to mention the guests' long journey home.","The room's chill deepened as the terrace doors opened. Cassian put the shawl over Mara's shoulders. His faithfulness to this small ritual had none of the intrusiveness of a public claim. She leaned into him with easy openness. They had both had a bellyful of imposed grandeur.","“Home?” she asked.","He smiled. “A little indulgence sounds sensible.”","Behind them the moon's halo had faded. The guests continued dancing. Greatness, glamor and mediocrity could occupy the critics tomorrow; tonight Mara wanted the harmony of his hand in hers and the warmth beyond the door."],"questions":[],"wordCount":957,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0141\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：月亮舞会的失宠赞助人</div><h1>The Patron Has to Wait</h1><p Waistcoat","volume":"婚后外传：公爵背后裂开的马甲","anchor":"chapter-0142","paragraphs":["Cassian's waistcoat was too small. Mara blamed the tailor, the tailor blamed Cassian's physique, and Rowan suggested surrender. The waistcoat's trademark embroidery had been designed for a naval officer with a narrow chest. Its historical significance did not make it stretch.","“You have a weak point,” Mara told her husband.","“Yes. You brought me here.”","They were helping a vocational guild hold a costume parade. The populous old port had a housing crisis, and the show would raise aid for displaced residents. Its sponsors from the tourism industry wanted spectacle; the guild wanted paid apprenticeships. Mara hoped those aims could be complementary rather than rivals. Notwithstanding the abundance of goodwill, money remained short.","The guild's head, Ada, valued practicality. Her proficiency at a sewing machine was famous. She could make two parallel seams seem to be one, match a faded tint and restore the symmetry of a crushed sleeve. She had volunteered to alter the waistcoat. Its thinness made repair difficult; excessive tension would tear it. Cassian's aspiration to breathe counted as another consideration.","One quirk of Ada's workroom was a bell that rang when anyone opened the wrong cupboard. Cassian triggered it twice. Her pursuit of predictability had by no means eliminated silliness. Mara found an old vice beneath the workbench and used it to steady a loose bracket. The simple tool's efficacy was welcome amid the room's complexity.","A promoter watched with impatience. Productivity, he announced, required uniformity. All costumes should fit identical mannequins. Ada asked how this would improve their suitability for actual people. The prevailing ideal of a single physique was as questionable as the prevalence of plastic copies in his shop. His promised simplicity concealed a practical disadvantage.","The apprentices had given their time voluntarily. Their aspiration deserved better than randomly assigned work or an unattractive uniform. Ada would strive to match tasks to skills. The promoter's vague assertion that everyone should simply work faster ignored accessibility, fatigue and the needs of beginners. It was similar to putting a faster motor in a machine with a broken belt.","The promoter promised transferability of the design files, but the apprentices could open none of them on their machines. Ada had already asked about this apparent triviality. Mara watched the unanswered email join a growing stack. A new computer would add to the cost, not solve the supplier's evasiveness.","Mara admired Ada's realism. She had no desire to conquer a runway through starvation or perfect conformity. The promoter called caution unattractive. Cassian responded with enough aristocratic civility to make the insult seem very foolish. His apparent calm did not diminish the severity of the warning.","He remained wise enough not to make a speech, plus the waistcoat made breathing difficult. Still, he wanted to elicit an explanation. Treating exhaustion as a sin encouraged concealment; calling cruelty discipline did not make it less inhumane. Ada's commitment had a constancy he admired. The promoter's arrogance seemed designed to awaken the opposite response.","The room contained a mechanical astronomy display borrowed from the maritime museum. A crank made model planets revolve. Its older companion depicted the geo-centric view; a later model placed the sun at the centre. Ada had used the two as an analogue for the fashion industry's changing criteria. Mara found that amusing. The promoter did not wish to hear that his standards might have a history.","The display's complexity did not fit with the tiny label beside it. Ada had tried to add to the explanation, but the promoter feared visitors would not read. 'I do not agree at all,' Mara said. People could enjoy beauty and an interesting question together. A luxuriant border of painted leaves made the dry text surprisingly inviting.","He claimed his selection process had scientific rigour. Ada asked for its basis. He said measurements had been chosen randomly to avoid bias. Randomly chosen measurements would not necessarily produce useful clothes, she answered. Their validity depended on what they were meant to establish. The promoter changed the subject to the rarity of his fabric.","She disliked the certificate's deceptiveness. It hid its lack of source information beneath formal language. Truthfulness required a maker's record, not a bigger seal. A possible instance of corruption deserved checking; random accusations of deceit did not help. Cassian asked for the record with such smooth civility that the promoter could not easily refuse. The only drawback was having to wait.","Mara touched it. A new chemical finish made it sparkle; its old label claimed it was untreated silk. The similarity of two swatches did not settle their composition. Their difference mattered for conservation. She requested a sample record before attaching the fabric to an original dress. It was prudence, not a sin against spontaneity.","The promoter accused her of prudery because she had questioned the dress's cut. She had done no such thing. Her concern was the fastening's strength, not how much skin it showed. Cassian stood beside her, painfully constrained by the waistcoat. His silence made the scene funnier than a speech.","Ada clipped the final thread. A succession of apprentices began lining up, their stamina already strained by preparations. Mara took utmost care with a damaged sleeve. The ultimate goal was a safe show, not the promoter's superiority over everyone in the room. His perpetual competitiveness could overwhelm the volunteers if nobody interrupted it.","A snap came from the temporary runway. Someone had removed a support to create storage space beneath it. Mara saw an apprentice step towards the gap. “Stop.”","The word cut through the room. Cassian caught the girl's elbow. Ada braced the rail. Its bulk shifted, then held. For one second the room seemed perfectly still.","Then Rowan said, “Damn the waistcoat. Carry her.”"],"questions":[],"wordCount":951,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0142\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵背后裂开的马甲</div><h1>The Man in the Borrowed Waistcoat</h1><p id=\"chapter-0142-p1\">Cassian&#x27;s waistcoat <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-792\" data-words=\"792\">was</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8567\" data-words=\"8567\">too</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7661\" data-words=\"7661\">small</a>.
